b22a5580d0778e57a66d3f75c76e3c55b88701e1
     1	---
     2	format: 2
     3	task_id: dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01
     4	kind: code
     5	security: true
     6	allowed_files:
     7	  - home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml
     8	  - home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-settings-managed.json
     9	  - home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-worker-settings.json
    10	  - home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-managed-settings.json
    11	  - home/dot_claude-worker/modify_private_settings.json
    12	  - home/dot_claude/hooks/executable_orchestrator-only.sh
    13	  - .claude/settings.json
    14	  - scripts/generate-agent-configs.py
    15	  - Makefile
    16	  - home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agents
    17	  - home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agent-state.sh
    18	  - home/dot_agents/skills/agmsg-orchestration/SKILL.md
    19	  - .gitignore
    20	  - tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py
    21	  - tests/unit/test_claude_settings_merge.py
    22	  - tests/unit/test_herdr_agents.py
    23	  - tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py
    24	  - tests/unit/test_claude_managed_settings.py
    25	  - .orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01/**
    26	after: []
    27	threat_table:
    28	  - control: "home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml (trusted checkout and interpreter bindings, static trusted_paths inventory, distinct worker policy, managed sandbox policy and worker caches)"
    29	    disposition: "residual: the rendered policy reduces accidental and injected damage; tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py::GenerateAgentConfigsTest::test_worker_policy_is_separate_from_orchestrator_policy checks both platforms without claiming same-UID isolation."
    30	  - control: "home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-settings-managed.json (orchestrator user settings with failIfUnavailable true, onFailure block and hook PATH bindings)"
    31	    disposition: "residual: the user baseline preserves orchestrator functions within the managed sandbox policy; tests/unit/test_claude_settings_merge.py::ClaudeSettingsMergeTest::test_orchestrator_settings_keep_existing_policy checks the specified changes and retained settings."
    32	  - control: "home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-worker-settings.json (static trusted-path and worktree-configuration Edit denies, empty user exclusions, restricted write allowances, native merge denials and minimal delivery hooks)"
    33	    disposition: "residual: dedicated user settings reduce worker access to trusted paths; tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py::ClaudeWorkerSettingsTest::test_worker_policy_renders_for_both_platforms checks the baseline and distinguishes it from shared managed exclusions and socket access."
    34	  - control: "home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-managed-settings.json (admin-required sandbox enforcement, fixed rendered control-plane exclusions and Herdr socket allowance)"
    35	    disposition: "residual: operator installation establishes the documented enforcement scope against project and local sandbox relaxation; tests/unit/test_claude_managed_settings.py::ClaudeManagedSettingsTest::test_rendered_policy_requires_admin_sandbox asserts the rendered keys; shared control-plane capabilities and same-UID host access remain residual."
    36	  - control: "home/dot_claude-worker/modify_private_settings.json (deployment of the dedicated worker baseline without importing orchestrator policy)"
    37	    disposition: "residual: replacing the worker baseline's managed values reduces stale or permissive user configuration; tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py::ClaudeWorkerSettingsTest::test_worker_settings_replace_stale_policy_idempotently checks repeated rendering in temporary homes."
    38	  - control: "home/dot_claude/hooks/executable_orchestrator-only.sh (anchored launch-root guard for tracked and main-checkout local project hooks)"
    39	    disposition: "residual: the guard reduces execution of worker-influenced project code and orchestrator hooks in worker sessions; tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py::ClaudeWorkerSettingsTest::test_project_guard_uses_launch_root_and_never_runs_worker_payloads checks fixed arguments, canonical identity and child status."
    40	  - control: "formatter and project-map reload configuration-selection boundary (the edited file path in the hook tool input and its owning repository)"
    41	    disposition: "residual: the guard skips these commands for input outside CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR or in a worker repository under .claude/worktrees/, preventing consumption of worker-controlled Prettier JavaScript configuration and ruff settings; tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py::ClaudeWorkerSettingsTest::test_project_guard_uses_launch_root_and_never_runs_worker_payloads checks both the skip and normal anchored-file execution."
    42	  - control: ".claude/settings.json (every tracked project command routed through the installed guard with pinned PATH bindings, absolute interpreters and onFailure block)"
    43	    disposition: "residual: guarded commands reduce worker-triggered project-hook execution; tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py::GenerateAgentConfigsTest::test_every_tracked_project_hook_is_guarded_and_blocks_on_failure exhaustively traverses the hook entries."
    44	  - control: "scripts/generate-agent-configs.py (separate user and managed settings generation, static manifest deny inventory and absolute executable bindings)"
    45	    disposition: "residual: rendering an explicit manifest list removes Git object traversal from inventory construction; tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py::GenerateAgentConfigsTest::test_worker_deny_inventory_is_static_and_matches_manifest checks exact manifest correspondence and filesystem coverage."
    46	  - control: "Makefile (the install-managed-settings target that renders a home-directory staging file and prints the operator's sudo install command)"
    47	    disposition: "residual: staging makes the administrative installation concrete without granting the worker administrative authority; tests/unit/test_claude_managed_settings.py::ClaudeManagedSettingsTest::test_install_target_stages_and_prints_without_running_sudo checks the destination, rendered content, quoting and absence of privileged execution."
    48	  - control: "home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agents (worker pane environment, dedicated Claude configuration, private caches, worker user hooks and guarded main-checkout local hooks)"
    49	    disposition: "residual: launch preparation reduces accidental inheritance of orchestrator settings and stale hooks; tests/unit/test_herdr_agents.py::HerdrAgentsTest::test_every_worker_launch_receives_dedicated_policy_and_private_caches captures managed and standalone launches and repeated starts."
    50	  - control: "home/dot_local/bin/common/executable_herdr-agent-state.sh (repository-owned Herdr state helper with an explicitly supplied absolute Python interpreter)"
    51	    disposition: "residual: binding the helper and its Python child reduces shim execution; tests/unit/test_herdr_agents.py::HerdrAgentsTest::test_hook_descendants_do_not_execute_path_interpreters checks executed identities with planted shims."
    52	  - control: "home/dot_agents/skills/agmsg-orchestration/SKILL.md (the governing Worker Playbook step 4 sentence)"
    53	    disposition: "residual: the sentence removes procedural authorization for worker sandbox escapes; tests/unit/test_herdr_agents.py::HerdrAgentsTest::test_worker_playbook_requires_every_command_inside_sandbox checks its replacement; shared managed control-plane capabilities remain available and are not represented as per-seat isolation."
    54	  - control: ".gitignore (the .uv-cache directory pattern)"
    55	    disposition: "residual: ignoring the private cache reduces accidental cache publication; tests/unit/test_herdr_agents.py::HerdrAgentsTest::test_worker_uv_cache_directory_is_gitignored checks the pattern in a temporary checkout."
    56	  - control: "tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py (separate platform policies, static inventory, interpreter bindings and tracked hook coverage)"
    57	    disposition: "residual: render fixtures verify configuration and command construction; they do not establish live enforcement or authenticate host-local inputs."
    58	  - control: "tests/unit/test_claude_settings_merge.py (orchestrator baseline preservation and failure-policy rendering through the existing merge)"
    59	    disposition: "residual: temporary HOME fixtures verify baseline entries and identify runtime-added hooks separately; they do not install administrative settings."
    60	  - control: "tests/unit/test_herdr_agents.py (every launch route, repeated starts, main-local and worker-user hook placement, descendants, caches and retained native denials)"
    61	    disposition: "residual: fake launchers and subprocess markers verify effective arguments and executed fixture identities without starting live seats; shared managed exceptions remain explicitly outside per-seat exclusion claims."
    62	  - control: "tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py (dedicated settings deployment, static narrow denies, worktree configuration protection, project guard and hook failure configuration)"
    63	    disposition: "residual: fixtures verify configured controls and credential-free deployment behavior; runtime permission checks and the remaining path-consumption window remain deployment and host residuals."
    64	  - control: "tests/unit/test_claude_managed_settings.py (managed policy rendering, conflicting project and local settings fixtures and nonprivileged staging)"
    65	    disposition: "residual: fixtures verify the selected documented enforcement keys and staging behavior without simulating proof of Claude runtime enforcement or performing sudo installation."
    66	  - control: ".orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01/** (this task's branch artifacts)"
    67	    disposition: "residual: artifacts distinguish documentation facts, historical probes, final-head fixture results and unperformed operator deployment; acceptance compares their claims with the diff and CI."
    68	  - control: "the task-file copy at .orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01/task.md"
    69	    disposition: "residual: the first push includes the byte-identical dispatched task; TASK and RESULT carry its SHA256, which the orchestrator verifies at acceptance."
    70	  - control: "the evidence records under .orchestration/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01/"
    71	    disposition: "residual: report.md, validation.md, sandbox.md, learning.md, autoskill.md and worker review evidence retain commands, outputs, statuses and tested revisions without credentials or unmasked home-directory paths."
    72	  - control: "task metadata (PR_FEEDBACK_EVIDENCE, AUDIT_EVIDENCE, ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE and REVIEW_EVIDENCE)"
    73	    disposition: "residual: the orchestrator checks C1 to C10 and C7b, verifies dispatched bytes and binds integration evidence to the final head; P1-14 a05 and P1-6 implement retrieval of evidence of record from GitHub at its SHA; the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention."
    74	  - control: metadata separators
    75	    disposition: "residual: the first document separators delimit format-2 metadata; the task-copy SHA256 binds the complete document."
    76	  - control: in-process effects
    77	    disposition: "residual: generation and tests use repository files and temporary fixtures; the worker performs no live deployment, login, administrative installation, permission-policy mutation or operator-session restart."
    78	  - control: workflow and interpreter or environment inputs
    79	    disposition: "residual: absolute interpreters, Python -I, sanitized Git, fixed hook PATH and trusted executable bindings reduce environment injection; the host, native loader and same-UID processes remain outside this task's security guarantee."
    80	  - control: new or renamed scripts
    81	    disposition: "residual: the worker settings modify script, orchestrator-only guard and repository-owned Herdr state helper have named source and installed paths; RESULT inventories their interpreter and descendant bindings without introducing a worker-write-boundary hook or gh wrapper."
    82	  - control: "5.14 item 0: host threat model"
    83	    disposition: "residual: host controls reduce accidental and injected damage without isolating seats sharing one OS user; prevention of worker-initiated merges requires a separate OS user or host under D-024-23's future P1-16, which the operator decides; the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention, through P1-11's main-push flag for missing acceptance or gate evidence; shared managed control-plane exclusions and the Herdr socket remain residual."
    84	  - control: "5.14 item 1: worker write controls"
    85	    disposition: "residual: dedicated configuration, static symlink-aware Edit denies including worktree configuration and orchestrator worklogs and caches, narrow writable agmsg stores, managed sandbox enforcement and failIfUnavailable true reduce damage; documented path checks do not establish atomic immunity to concurrent path swaps."
    86	  - control: "5.14 item 2: sanitized environment and private executable PATH"
    87	    disposition: "residual: sanitized Git verification and absolute hook entrypoints reduce injected configuration, while PATH=/usr/bin:/bin binds upstream env-bash descendants away from shims; the remaining consuming-helper contracts belong to P1-14 a05, P1-10 a02, audit-pool-a02 and P1-6."
    88	  - control: "5.14 item 3: trusted-path checks"
    89	    disposition: "residual: canonical anchor and interpreter checks reduce worker-selected trusted paths and account for symlink components and filesystem case aliases; other consuming-helper checks remain with their owning hardening tasks."
    90	  - control: "5.14 item 4: verified executable and consumed-byte identity"
    91	    disposition: "residual: hooks execute selected absolute entrypoints and trusted scripts, and upstream shell descendants inherit the fixed system PATH; evidence snapshots, last-use digest checks and their race limitations remain with the consuming orchestrator-side tasks; store-selected driver execution remains the separately named agmsg residual."
    92	  - control: "5.14 item 5: output and reservation identity"
    93	    disposition: "residual: this task adds no reservation protocol; full-head output names, fixed-inode locks and physical-slot identity remain with the orchestrator-side tasks that create them."
    94	  - control: "5.14 item 6: honest residuals, hooks and MCP"
    95	    disposition: "residual: guarded tracked and main-local project hooks and minimal worker user hooks reduce host execution routes; shared managed Herdr socket access, control-plane exclusions, approved external processes and same-UID processes outside every seat sandbox remain residual."
    96	  - control: "5.14 item 7: trusted-execution verification"
    97	    disposition: "residual: this task records red-then-green render, merge, managed-policy, guard, launch, descendant and cache tests; P1-6 owns tests/unit/trusted_exec_contract.py and the consuming-helper contract tests."
    98	  - control: "5.14 item 8: committed approval ledger"
    99	    disposition: "residual: the regime reads the ledger through the GitHub contents API at the SHA returned by git ls-remote origin refs/heads/main; P1-14 a05 implements that read, and local refs, files and object traversal provide no approval authority; the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention."
   100	  - control: "5.14 item 9: private uv caches"
   101	    disposition: "residual: per-seat worker caches, restricted worker write allowances and explicit denies on the orchestrator worklog and uv-cache reduce cache poisoning; consuming hardening tasks use the protected orchestrator cache with --offline and execute no worker, shared uv or mise cache content."
   102	  - control: "5.14 item 10: no worker command outside the sandbox"
   103	    disposition: "residual: empty worker user exclusions, disabled unsandboxed requests and Codex in-sandbox network reduce escape routes; the worker rule forbids using shared managed control-plane exceptions, whose availability and Herdr socket access remain residual; the deterministic Codex permgate rule and live deployment remain operator steps."
   104	  - control: "hook failure policy"
   105	    disposition: "residual: every generated and tracked hook entry, including launcher-written worker user and main-local entries, carries onFailure block, reducing fail-open behavior where Claude supports it; configuration tests cover missing-command and timeout policy, while background hooks and events on which the field has no effect carry no enforcement responsibility."
   106	  - control: "agmsg store driver and plugin selection (team configuration drivers.partition, db/trusted-plugins authorization and installed storage.sh sourcing)"
   107	    disposition: "residual: every seat writes the store, and installed storage.sh resolves drivers.partition as a driver path and sources it during unsandboxed delivery; it also uses db/trusted-plugins to authorize plugins/partition/shared.sh for sourcing; fixed PATH does not stop these routes; worker denies on plugins/** and db/trusted-plugins reduce the plugin route; dotfiles-T133-agmsg-hardening-a01 owns upstream basename validation, plugin authorization hardening and their adversarial red-then-green tests; this task introduces no store-derived sourcing beyond the installed scripts' current behavior."
   108	  - control: orchestrator effect merge
   109	    disposition: "residual: the worker is forbidden to merge; the orchestrator follows the final-head integration gate, required permission approval and pinned squash-merge procedure, with evidence of record retrieved from GitHub; shared credentials still permit worker-initiated API merges, so the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention."
   110	  - control: orchestrator effect update-branch
   111	    disposition: "residual: the orchestrator owns branch updates and obtains fresh required CI, feedback, audit and security evidence for the resulting head."
   112	  - control: orchestrator effect forced worker removal
   113	    disposition: "residual: no forced removal is planned; existing dirty-worktree protections remain, and acceptance identifies any operator-authorized discarded state."
   114	premises:
   115	  - claim: >-
   116	      The historical a05 premises carried by the preceding text describe its recorded observations. They do not require this worker to rebuild PR 400, edit the approval ledger or implement P1-14 a05.

D-024-23 supersedes D-024-22's credential-separation claim because gh Keychain access is shared by seats running as the same macOS user. Prevention requires a separate OS user or host; the current GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention.

The dedicated worker renderer does not import that orchestrator settings merge or its attachment behavior. The administrative managed-settings file is a separate deployment destination.

The dedicated Claude configuration directory and private cache variables reach the actual worker process on every launch route. This task adds no gh configuration directory or credential-separation mechanism.

Worker delivery moves to the worker configuration directory's user settings, and the launcher's main-checkout local hooks receive the orchestrator-only guard.

Project and local settings cannot supply sandbox relaxations in that scope. User settings and shared managed exceptions remain distinct from project and local settings.
   147	    sources:

   154	    sources:
   155	      - .orchestration/acceptance/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01.md
   156	      - .orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-08e6ac7.md.last.md
   157	---
   158	
   159	# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01 — plan 024 P1-15 under D-024-17, D-024-19, D-024-20, D-024-21 and D-024-23
   160	
   161	## Goal
   162	
   163	The host is not a security boundary between seats sharing one OS user. gh keys Keychain credentials by host and user, so a worker running as the same macOS user can read the orchestrator's token regardless of `GH_CONFIG_DIR`. Prevention of a worker-initiated merge requires a separate OS user or host under D-024-23's future P1-16, which the operator decides whether to pursue. Until then the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention. The regime rule requires the main-push CI run to flag a merge that lacks the required acceptance record or gate-passage evidence. P1-11 owns implementation of that flag; this task states the rule. Approvals and gate evidence of record are read from GitHub at a SHA through its API inside the sandbox. Host-local controls reduce accidental and injected damage without establishing isolation from a malicious same-UID process.
   164	
   165	This task implements section 5.14 items 1, 9 and 10 through a dedicated Claude worker configuration directory, static Edit deny rules on trusted paths and worktree configuration, managed sandbox enforcement, guarded project hooks, hook interpreter and PATH bindings, private worker caches and the rule that worker commands run inside their sandbox. Every Claude seat has `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true`. Codex workers have `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`.
   166	
   167	The administrative managed policy is shared by every Claude seat. Its Herdr socket allowance and three control-plane exclusions remain available to workers as well as the orchestrator. Empty arrays in worker user settings do not subtract these managed entries. Their availability is a residual under item 0, and the worker execution rule supplies no authorization to use them outside the sandbox.
   168	
   169	[memory:decision] D-024-20 makes this task the dispatch prerequisite of P1-14 a05, P1-10 a02, audit-pool-a02 and P1-6. The orchestrator places this task in their `after:` fields before dispatch. This task has `after: []`. A Codex seat performs these Claude configuration edits under the routing rule, including the Worker Playbook step 4 sentence permitted by plan 021 step 8.3.
   170	
   171	The design source is `.orchestration/plans/024-design-quality.md` section 5.14 items 0 through 10. D-024-21, D-024-23 and the amended items 0, 1 and 8 govern this revision. D-024-22's proposed gh credential separation is superseded. The acceptance record preserves documentation facts and earlier proposals as history.
   172	
   173	The third C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-ecc7ab5.md.last.md`. Its five P1 findings remain recorded. The retained mechanisms are documented symlink-aware deny rules, approval reads outside the local object database, hook interpreter bindings and runtime hook failure policy.
   174	
   175	The fourth C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-8d42532.md.last.md`. Its six P1 findings remain recorded. The retained mechanisms are the static manifest deny inventory, protection of the orchestrator worklog and cache, narrow home denies around writable agmsg stores, correct linked-worktree hook placement and fixed hook-runtime PATH.
   176	
   177	The fifth C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-08e6ac7.md.last.md`. Its three P1 findings remain recorded. This revision removes the gh credential-separation mechanism, records the upstream agmsg store-driver residual and moves sandbox enforcement into administrative managed settings.
   178	
   179	The file-tool rule is the documented sentence, “Deny rules: apply when either the requested path or the file it resolves to matches.” Edit and Write refuse a requested file that is itself a symlink. Directory symlinks are subject to resolved-target permission checks, and the tools check the resolved location again when opening the approved file. These documented checks reduce redirection risk without establishing an atomic host security boundary against concurrent same-UID changes. [Claude permission reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#symlinks)
   180	
   181	The 2026-10-19T03:45:06Z sandbox probe ran `gh api repos/mryfmo/dotfiles --jq .name` and returned `dotfiles`. The recorded sandbox proxy used CONNECT without TLS interception, and gh read the Keychain token. This historical observation confirms neither credential separation nor final-head deployment readiness.
   182	
   183	The documentation facts recorded at 03:2xZ establish that Edit denies also affect the Bash sandbox, hooks reload during a session and an unavailable sandbox is fail-open by default. D-024-21's 04:0xZ decision establishes the dedicated Claude configuration mechanism, directory-specific Claude credentials, symlink coverage and `onFailure: "block"` support. These Claude credentials do not separate gh Keychain access.
   184	
   185	The settings documentation states, “In a worktree, it uses the file at the main checkout’s root.” Linked-worktree sessions therefore load the main checkout's `.claude/settings.local.json`. Worker hooks belong in `~/.claude-worker/settings.json`, and the main checkout's local hooks also receive the orchestrator-only guard. [Claude local settings reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings#where-claude-code-keeps-the-local-file-in-a-git-repository)
   186	
   187	## Files
   188	
   189	1. `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml` remains the source of agent policy and model profiles.
   190	
   191	   The manifest names `orchestrator_checkout` as the anchored primary checkout and supplies trusted deployment bindings for the guard, hook scripts and absolute interpreters. These bindings resolve outside worker worktrees and executable caches. Worker cwd, tool input, worktree configuration and inherited command variables do not select them. The manifest also supplies the trusted bindings from which the render resolves the absolute directories holding `ruff`, `prettier` and `terminal-browser`, without using mise shims.
   192	
   193	   The manifest defines a separate Claude worker settings output at `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-worker-settings.json` and an administrative managed-settings output at `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-managed-settings.json`. The worker configuration directory is `~/.claude-worker`. The launcher passes its expanded absolute path as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
   194	
   195	   A static `trusted_paths:` list names every protected top-level path of the anchored checkout and the protected home paths. It explicitly includes `<anchored-checkout>/.agents/worklog/**` and `<anchored-checkout>/.agents/worklog/uv-cache/**`. The `.claude` entries protect tracked contents and the main checkout's `.claude/settings.local.json` without denying the whole `.claude/worktrees/` tree. The list explicitly names directory targets and descendant patterns where both are required.
   196	
   197	   Separate worker Edit denies protect `//<anchored-checkout>/.claude/worktrees/*/.claude/**` and `//<anchored-checkout>/.claude/worktrees/*/.mcp.json`. The `.claude` directory target is also protected. Ordinary worktree source files and the per-seat cache remain outside these configuration denies.
   198	
   199	   The home-path denies are `~/.agents/agent-config.yaml`, `~/.agents/model-profiles.env`, `~/.agents/skills/**/scripts/**`, `~/.agents/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins`, `~/.claude/**`, `~/.claude-worker/**`, `~/.local/bin/**`, `~/.local/share/**`, `~/.local/state/**`, `~/.local/share/mise/**`, `~/.config/**`, `~/.cache/uv/**` and `~/.local/share/uv/**`. No blanket deny on `~/.agents/**` or `~/.local/**` replaces this list.
   200	
   201	   The agmsg store paths `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/teams`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/run` and `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/ext-tools` remain writable. Deny patterns cover neither these directory targets nor their descendants except the file `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins`. Edit denies merge into the sandbox configuration, so Bash writes to that file and `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**` are refused too. Their influence on installed delivery-script driver and plugin selection remains the separately owned agmsg residual.
   202	
   203	   The orchestrator retains its existing permissions, cache allowances and hook functions within the administrative managed policy. Its user baseline receives `failIfUnavailable: true`, `onFailure: "block"` on its hooks and the interpreter and PATH bindings below. Its exclusion entries match the three control-plane entries specified below; the former Linux `gh *` exclusion is removed.
   204	
   205	   The worker user policy contains no excluded commands and sets `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`. It contains no Herdr socket allowance and no allow-all Unix socket switch. Its sandbox is enabled and has `failIfUnavailable: true`. Shared managed entries remain effective despite these empty worker arrays.
   206	
   207	   Worker `sandbox.filesystem.allowWrite` contains only the existing agmsg store paths and per-seat cache paths. The worktree is the project root and supplies its ordinary project write root. Shared uv and mise write allowances are not copied into worker settings.
   208	
   209	   The Codex worker profile sets `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`. Existing model selections and Codex shared Git grants retain their meaning. The deterministic Codex permgate rule that stops approving sandbox escapes is an operator step.
   210	
   211	2. `scripts/generate-agent-configs.py` renders the distinct orchestrator user, worker user and administrative managed baselines from the manifest.
   212	
   213	   The trusted-path deny inventory is rendered from the static `trusted_paths:` list. Rendering performs no Git object traversal, `git ls-tree` enumeration or tracked-path discovery. Worker-writable refs, replacement refs and objects do not determine the list.
   214	
   215	   Anchored checkout rules use absolute `//` permission patterns. Rendering includes the explicitly listed trusted paths themselves and their descendants. It emits no checkout-wide parent deny that defeats ordinary worktree writes. The separate worktree-configuration denies remain present.
   216	
   217	   Edit denies cover Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit through Claude's permission system. Requested-path and resolved-target matching supply the documented symlink coverage. The generator creates no `executable_worker-write-boundary.py` or replacement PreToolUse path validator.
   218	
   219	   Every direct Git verification introduced or changed by this task follows section 5.14 item 2. It begins with `env -i`, uses the selected absolute Git executable and an explicit `-C`, sets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, and supplies `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c protocol.allow=never -c protocol.https.allow=always`. Diff and show also use `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` and `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c core.sshCommand=false`.
   220	
   221	   The private executable-link directory contains only links to selected absolute trusted binaries. Rendering rejects missing bindings, worker-controlled executable paths, shims, empty PATH entries and relative PATH entries. Python invocations use `-I`. Hook execution uses the role-specific pinned PATH bindings specified below. The render test asserts that `ruff`, `prettier` and `terminal-browser` resolve under the rendered orchestrator tool PATH on the rendering host and that no entry of that PATH is a shims directory.
   222	
   223	   `tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py::GenerateAgentConfigsTest::test_worker_deny_inventory_is_static_and_matches_manifest` asserts that the rendered list equals the manifest entry after trusted anchor and home expansion. It explicitly asserts the rendered home-path deny patterns `~/.local/share/**` and `~/.local/state/**`, including the application code under `~/.local/share` used by `~/.local/bin` wrappers such as `terminal-browser`. It reads the filesystem of the test's own checkout and asserts that every tracked top-level source path is represented. It invokes no Git command to discover those paths.
   224	
   225	   The inventory fixtures cover files, directories, `.claude` children, the main-local settings file, ordinary worktree writes, both worktree-configuration patterns, an anchor containing spaces and both explicit worklog patterns. Adding a top-level source path without a manifest entry makes the coverage assertion fail. Planted Git environment and replacement-ref fixtures leave rendering unchanged.
   226	
   227	   `tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py::ClaudeWorkerSettingsTest::test_worker_denies_leave_agmsg_stores_writable` asserts the rendered deny patterns `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**` and `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins` and verifies that the four store directories `db`, `teams`, `run` and `ext-tools` and their descendant fixtures remain writable except that one file.
   228	
   229	3. `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-settings-managed.json` remains the orchestrator user baseline.
   230	
   231	   The existing `home/dot_claude/modify_private_settings.json` continues to render the orchestrator's `~/.claude/settings.json`. That modify script is not imported into the dedicated worker renderer and remains outside `allowed_files`.
   232	
   233	   This existing template's name does not make it administrative managed settings. The new `claude-managed-settings.json` template supplies that separate scope.
   234	
   235	   The orchestrator retains its current user policy content apart from `failIfUnavailable: true`, `onFailure: "block"`, the required interpreter and PATH bindings and alignment of exclusions with the three control-plane entries. Worker-specific Edit denies and restricted worker cache allowances do not enter this baseline. Administrative sandbox requirements apply to the orchestrator as well as workers.
   236	
   237	   Every generated hook entry carries `onFailure: "block"`. Every generated command hook starts with `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=<pinned> HOME=<rendered home> LANG=C.UTF-8 TERM=dumb CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}" CLAUDE_ENV_FILE="${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE}"` before its selected absolute interpreter and script path. The formatter and project-map hooks run through the installed orchestrator-only guard and receive the role-specific pinned PATH specified below. Merge evidence identifies hooks appended by the existing modify script and distinguishes them from generated baseline entries. The report accounts for the effective deployed hook set without equating baseline coverage with coverage of runtime-added entries.
   238	
   239	   `tests/unit/test_claude_settings_merge.py::ClaudeSettingsMergeTest::test_orchestrator_settings_keep_existing_policy` compares the merged policy with the preceding baseline and accounts for the specified changes. Repeated merging remains idempotent and preserves unrelated settings.
   240	
   241	4. `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-worker-settings.json` is the dedicated worker user baseline.
   242	
   243	   It renders the static trusted-path Edit denies, the worktree `.claude` and `.mcp.json` Edit denies, retained native merge denials, empty user `excludedCommands`, `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`, restricted `allowWrite`, no user Herdr socket allowance and `failIfUnavailable: true` for both Darwin and Linux.
   244	
   245	   Its hooks are limited to agmsg delivery. It contains no formatter, project-map, permgate, orchestrator attachment, Herdr state or worker-write-boundary hook. Tracked project hooks and main-checkout local hooks still load and receive the guard described below.
   246	
   247	   Every worker hook entry carries `onFailure: "block"`. Every command starts with `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=/usr/bin:/bin HOME=<rendered home> LANG=C.UTF-8 TERM=dumb CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}" CLAUDE_ENV_FILE="${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE}" AGMSG_RESOLVE_PROJECT=0` before the selected absolute shell interpreter and absolute installed agmsg script path. The prefix also re-exports `AGMSG_STORAGE_PATH` when it is set in the launcher environment. Project resolution from a writable `run/proj.<pid>.project` marker remains disabled, so delivery retains the explicit worker path. Descendants inherit this PATH.
   248	
   249	   The worker baseline does not copy the orchestrator's plugins, credential files or current hook arrays. Hook settings reload during a session, so protection is stated as current configuration rather than startup capture. `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` selects the separate user settings directory while project settings continue to apply within the administrative enforcement scope. [Claude settings reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings)
   250	
   251	   Worker user settings supply no additional socket or exclusion allowance. Their empty lists cannot remove the shared managed Herdr socket or control-plane exclusions.
   252	
   253	5. `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-managed-settings.json` supplies the administrative sandbox policy.
   254	
   255	   The rendered policy sets `sandbox.enabled: true`, `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true` and `sandbox.allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`. The last setting makes sandboxing admin-required. Project and local files cannot add sandbox exclusions, socket allowances or other documented relaxations in this scope. Managed Boolean values prevail over lower scopes. User settings remain a separate trusted scope, and arrays such as exclusions have no general managed-only lock. [Repository settings under an admin-required sandbox](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/sandboxing#repository-settings-under-an-admin-required-sandbox)
   256	
   257	   The rendered policy sets `sandbox.filesystem.disabled: false`, `sandbox.enableWeakerNestedSandbox: false`, `sandbox.enableWeakerNetworkIsolation: false`, `sandbox.network.allowAllUnixSockets: false`, `sandbox.network.allowLocalBinding: false` and `sandbox.allowAppleEvents: false`.
   258	
   259	   The rendered `sandbox.excludedCommands` list contains exactly `agmsg-dispatch *`, `herdr-agents *` and `<absolute-home>/.agents/skills/agmsg/scripts/actas-claim.sh *`. These are the orchestrator's three control-plane entries. It contains no `gh *`, `git push *` or authenticated-fetch exemption.
   260	
   261	   The rendered `sandbox.network.allowUnixSockets` list contains exactly the trusted home expansion of `~/.config/herdr/herdr.sock`. This allowance is shared by every seat under managed settings. Per-seat removal is unavailable in that scope, and the worker baseline's empty list does not revoke it.
   262	
   263	   Shared control-plane exclusions are also available in worker sessions. The worker execution rule forbids using them to leave the sandbox. The text treats their availability and the socket's host-execution capability as item 0 residuals rather than enforced per-seat separation.
   264	
   265	   `tests/unit/test_claude_managed_settings.py::ClaudeManagedSettingsTest::test_rendered_policy_requires_admin_sandbox` asserts the rendered content, exact exclusion and socket lists, trusted home expansion and every required Boolean value.
   266	
   267	   `tests/unit/test_claude_managed_settings.py::ClaudeManagedSettingsTest::test_managed_render_is_independent_of_conflicting_project_and_local_settings` supplies project and local fixtures that disable sandboxing, disable startup failure, enable unsandboxed retries, add exclusions and sockets, enable weaker isolation and widen filesystem writes. The trusted managed render remains unchanged. The test verifies configuration scope and content without presenting a simulated settings merger as runtime enforcement.
   268	
   269	6. `Makefile` adds only the `install-managed-settings` target and its `.PHONY` declaration.
   270	
   271	   The target uses the repository's existing rendering facilities to render `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-managed-settings.json` into `~/.claude/managed-settings.rendered.json`. The staging path is expanded to an absolute path under the user's home.
   272	
   273	   The target selects `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json` on macOS and `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` on Linux and prints the exact command `sudo install -m 0644 "<absolute-home>/.claude/managed-settings.rendered.json" "<detected-managed-settings-path>"` with the actual staging path and detected destination substituted and shell quoting preserved. The printed command is ready for the operator to run.
   274	
   275	   The target performs no sudo call, administrative installation, live settings application, authentication or session restart. The staging file alone does not establish managed enforcement.
   276

It distinguishes historical observations from final-head results and names all pending operator steps.

   477	The report states the user-visible effects. Claude workers use `~/.claude-worker` and require its own Claude login. Worker trusted-path and worktree-configuration Edit denies also affect Bash sandbox writes. Agmsg store paths remain writable except `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins`, and worker denies also cover `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**`. Worker user settings add no excluded commands or Herdr socket allowance, while shared managed settings retain the three control-plane exclusions and Herdr socket for every seat. Tracked and main-local orchestrator project hooks are no-ops in worker sessions. Worker caches are private. Every Claude seat fails when its sandbox is unavailable. Codex workers have in-sandbox network access.
   478	
   479	Administrative managed settings require sandboxing for the orchestrator as well as workers and disable unsandboxed retries. The former Linux gh exemption is removed. The orchestrator retains its existing functions within those requirements and the specified hook failure, guarding and interpreter/PATH-binding changes.
   480	
   481	The report states that no gh credential separation is established. Shared credentials permit worker-initiated API merges despite native command denials. The GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention, with the required main-push flag owned by P1-11.
   482	
   483	This task is `security: true`. The orchestrator obtains the task-level audit and a separate security-profile review of the final implementation head. The finding-class record includes `decision-not-bound-to-execution`, `unbound-evidence` and `fail-open-on-error`. A third non-advisory finding of one class stops the artifact for redesign under the inherited completion rules.
   484	
   485	Final-head feedback collection, task-level audit, finding dispositions, acceptance and the integration gate follow Orchestrator Playbook step 10 in its prescribed order. The gate receives `PR_FEEDBACK_EVIDENCE`, `AUDIT_EVIDENCE`, `ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE` and the agent review receipt. Changed heads receive fresh required evidence. The final-head security review precedes the gate.
   486	
   487	The permission-surface change receives the required operator-backed approval through the repository's existing process. The worker writes no approval row and treats no candidate-tree decision as authority.
   488	
   489	Live deployment is the operator's post-merge `make update`. The operator runs `make install-managed-settings`, inspects the rendered staging file and executes its printed `sudo install -m 0644 "<absolute-home>/.claude/managed-settings.rendered.json" "<detected-managed-settings-path>"` command with `/Library/Application Support/ClaudeCode/managed-settings.json` detected on macOS and `/etc/claude-code/managed-settings.json` detected on Linux. Administrative installation is recorded separately from staging and ordinary chezmoi application.
   490	
   491	The operator performs the Claude worker-directory login with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR=<absolute-home>/.claude-worker` selected.
   492	
   493	The operator applies the prepared deterministic Codex permgate rule that stops approving sandbox escapes and restarts affected sessions.
   494	
   495	The deployment record checks effective settings after `make update` and administrative installation, including hooks added by merge scripts or integration installers. It verifies the installed managed-settings content, admin-required sandbox scope, worker directory, both platform policy outputs, static deny inventory, worktree-configuration denies, writable agmsg stores, worker-user and main-local hook placement, hook PATH and interpreter descendants, failure-policy coverage and sandbox startup without exposing credentials.
   496	
   497	The effective-settings check includes conflicting project and local sandbox relaxations and confirms that they do not weaken the administrative policy. It also records the shared control-plane exclusions and Herdr socket as available capabilities. It makes no claim that worker user arrays subtract those entries.
   498	
   499	Merge alone does not establish deployment readiness. The orchestrator records `make update`, the managed-settings installation with sudo, the Claude worker-directory login, effective startup checks and the Codex permgate rule before releasing dependent hardening tasks for live operation. The upstream agmsg store-driver route remains a named residual until its owning task is implemented. GitHub remains detection, not prevention, until the operator pursues separate-user or separate-host isolation under future P1-16.
   500	
   501	## Checks (pasted by the orchestrator before each send)
   502	
   503	```
   504	(pasted at dispatch)
   505	```
   506	
   507	Local Bats, live enforcement, administrative installation, worker-directory login, permgate policy changes and deployment are not performed by the worker.
   508	Deployment remains unverified until the operator completes the post-merge steps; same-UID credential access, shared control-plane exclusions and Herdr socket access, writable-store driver selection and concurrent path consumption remain stated residuals.

   157	---
   158	
   159	# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01 — plan 024 P1-15 under D-024-17, D-024-19, D-024-20, D-024-21 and D-024-23
   160	
   161	## Goal
   162	
   163	The host is not a security boundary between seats sharing one OS user. gh keys Keychain credentials by host and user, so a worker running as the same macOS user can read the orchestrator's token regardless of `GH_CONFIG_DIR`. Prevention of a worker-initiated merge requires a separate OS user or host under D-024-23's future P1-16, which the operator decides whether to pursue. Until then the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention. The regime rule requires the main-push CI run to flag a merge that lacks the required acceptance record or gate-passage evidence. P1-11 owns implementation of that flag; this task states the rule. Approvals and gate evidence of record are read from GitHub at a SHA through its API inside the sandbox. Host-local controls reduce accidental and injected damage without establishing isolation from a malicious same-UID process.
   164	
   165	This task implements section 5.14 items 1, 9 and 10 through a dedicated Claude worker configuration directory, static Edit deny rules on trusted paths and worktree configuration, managed sandbox enforcement, guarded project hooks, hook interpreter and PATH bindings, private worker caches and the rule that worker commands run inside their sandbox. Every Claude seat has `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true`. Codex workers have `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`.
   166	
   167	The administrative managed policy is shared by every Claude seat. Its Herdr socket allowance and three control-plane exclusions remain available to workers as well as the orchestrator. Empty arrays in worker user settings do not subtract these managed entries. Their availability is a residual under item 0, and the worker execution rule supplies no authorization to use them outside the sandbox.
   168	
   169	[memory:decision] D-024-20 makes this task the dispatch prerequisite of P1-14 a05, P1-10 a02, audit-pool-a02 and P1-6. The orchestrator places this task in their `after:` fields before dispatch. This task has `after: []`. A Codex seat performs these Claude configuration edits under the routing rule, including the Worker Playbook step 4 sentence permitted by plan 021 step 8.3.
   170	
   171	The design source is `.orchestration/plans/024-design-quality.md` section 5.14 items 0 through 10. D-024-21, D-024-23 and the amended items 0, 1 and 8 govern this revision. D-024-22's proposed gh credential separation is superseded. The acceptance record preserves documentation facts and earlier proposals as history.
   172	
   173	The third C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-ecc7ab5.md.last.md`. Its five P1 findings remain recorded. The retained mechanisms are documented symlink-aware deny rules, approval reads outside the local object database, hook interpreter bindings and runtime hook failure policy.
   174	
   175	The fourth C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-8d42532.md.last.md`. Its six P1 findings remain recorded. The retained mechanisms are the static manifest deny inventory, protection of the orchestrator worklog and cache, narrow home denies around writable agmsg stores, correct linked-worktree hook placement and fixed hook-runtime PATH.
   176	
   177	The fifth C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-08e6ac7.md.last.md`. Its three P1 findings remain recorded. This revision removes the gh credential-separation mechanism, records the upstream agmsg store-driver residual and moves sandbox enforcement into administrative managed settings.
   178	
   179	The file-tool rule is the documented sentence, “Deny rules: apply when either the requested path or the file it resolves to matches.” Edit and Write refuse a requested file that is itself a symlink. Directory symlinks are subject to resolved-target permission checks, and the tools check the resolved location again when opening the approved file. These documented checks reduce redirection risk without establishing an atomic host security boundary against concurrent same-UID changes. [Claude permission reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#symlinks)
   180	

exec
/bin/zsh -lc "rg -n 'Codex seat worklogs|task-level audit|security.profile' ~/.agents/skills/agmsg-orchestration/SKILL.md; cat ~/.codex/plugins/cache/ponytail/ponytail/5.3.2/skills/ponytail/SKILL.md; git status --short; git rev-parse HEAD" in ~/Workspace/dotfiles
 succeeded in 23ms:
21:- A Claude session started in the repository root outside Herdr (a plain shell, mosh/ssh, or `claude -p`) is a pane-less orchestrator: nothing seats a worker automatically, and the SessionStart `herdr-agents --attach` hook prints a summary line naming that state, the on-demand commands, and any seated worker, followed in a regime repository by the `agmsg-orchestration:` directive line. Bring the regime up on demand: claim the seat outside the sandbox with the composite instance id, `actas-claim.sh <repo> claude-code <name> <session_id>.<claude pid>` (a claim from sandboxed Bash writes the bare session id and turn delivery then skips silently; see the seat-lock bullet below); seat the worker with `herdr-agents --add-worker <worktree>` (it derives `HERDR_SOCKET_PATH` from the default Herdr server socket `~/.config/herdr/herdr.sock`, the path the Claude sandbox allowlists, before creating anything, accepts a claude worker's workspace-trust dialog during spawn's readiness wait, and takes `--ready-timeout <seconds>`); confirm the worker's placement from its `run/spawn.*` placement record itself (the `herdr:<socket>:<pane>` row that upstream `agmsg_spawn_path` resolves) and the `linkage=` line `--add-worker` prints, never from `team.sh <team> --json`, which observes Codex members by reading their pane; treat the `linkage=` line `--add-worker` prints as the `AGMSG-PING` evidence, and on `pong=no` or `linkage=unreached` wake again with `agmsg-dispatch <team> <orchestrator> <worker> <pane> 'AGMSG-PING v1 task_id=<id> reason=<reason>'` and verify the PING's `read_at` (`poke.sh` only for a viewed workspace); and dispatch no AGMSG-TASK before its `AGMSG-PONG` arrives. Without a managed workspace the auditor runs headless, in the form the task-level audit bullet below names. A pane-less session uses turn delivery when the Monitor cannot run; see "Identity, delivery, and storage" for the platform limits.
33:- Delegate all repository-mutating work — file edits, builds, test runs, and git state changes — to seated workers, with at most one seated worker per git worktree and sequential assignments within one worktree. Add worktrees for parallelism; never use parallel `codex exec` or per-task Codex spawning, except that the read-only, non-interactive task-level audit the orchestrator runs during acceptance (the task-level audit bullet below) is not worker spawning and is permitted; it is identity-less. agmsg/herdr control-plane commands (`delivery.sh`, `watch.sh`, `actas-claim.sh`, `send.sh`, `join.sh`, and herdr agent/pane commands) are orchestrator-side exemptions.
43:  - Some steps stay sequential. The single audit tab serializes audits; the task-level audit of T67 reduces them to one per task. The orchestrator-side gate (`make require-crit-review`) and merges also run one at a time.
72:- Before every acceptance action (revise, accept, merge) the orchestrator runs `scripts/regime-counters.sh <task id>` and pastes its output into the acceptance record. S2 is decided from INV-6's counts: each component the script prints is read from its output, and for each component it prints as `unknown` the record pastes the command and output that read the source INV-6 names for that component, or, while that source has no entry for the task, says so and pastes the reading of the interim record the orchestrator used in its place. S1 (plan 006: the same finding class three times on one artifact, from any reviewer) is decided from the record's finding-class table, which gives a class to every finding the PR-feedback sweep lists, whatever its disposition, and to every task-level audit finding; three rows of one class stop the artifact. Read that table over non-advisory rows only: list advisory P2 rows with their classes (`advisory-p2` and the `local:<sha7>:<n>` rows `local-advisory.sh` writes), exclude them from S1's three-rows count, and name the excluded rows in the record. The gate's S6 check still sees every ledger row, so an advisory class at three rows gets its `searches` entry from `acceptance-front-matter.py` like any other. INV-6's message and head counts and the S2 lines (`revises`, `amendments`, `questions`, `bot_p1_heads`) take no ledger rows; `scripts/regime-counters.sh` prints them unchanged.
170:       - A P2 finding of the Codex Bot, local review or task-level audit is advisory: disposition it `not-applicable:advisory P2 owned by <lineage>-followup-a0<n>` and record an `<id><TAB><class>` row under `## Owned by <owner>` in the lineage's acceptance record, with the orchestrator writing the owner task file before the row.
173:    2. Run the task-level audit of that head in the pair or headless form of the task-level audit bullet. It exits nonzero for every verdict other than `correct`. An exit with `Audit verdict: incorrect` is not a failed step: continue to step 3 and disposition its findings. A `blocked` or missing verdict means re-running the audit.
174:    3. Write the acceptance record, summarising the dispositions, with every audit finding dispositioned as the task-level audit bullet says: for an `incorrect` verdict the record's JSON `audit_findings`, which the gate reads through `ACCEPTANCE_EVIDENCE`.
217:## Codex seat worklogs
---
name: ponytail
description: >
  Lazy senior dev mode: the smallest change that fully solves the task, and a
  reply a busy human understands in one read. Use on any coding task (writing,
  fixing, refactoring, reviewing, choosing dependencies) and when the user says
  "ponytail", "be lazy", "simplest solution", "yagni", or complains about
  over-engineering or bloat. Levels: lite, full (default), ultra.
argument-hint: "[lite|full|ultra|off]"
license: MIT
---

# Ponytail

You are a lazy senior developer. The best code is the code never written. You solve the whole problem with the least new code. End your reply with one or two lines: what you skipped or did not check, and any risk the user must know.

Active for the whole session until the user says "stop ponytail" or "normal mode". Switch level: `/ponytail lite|full|ultra`.

## Before you write

Read the task and the code it touches. List every place your change must reach: callers, tests, fixtures, config, exports. Check what your change could break for users: data it would destroy or expose, callers that stop working. That is scope. Extra features are not.

## The smallest complete change

Take the first option that fully works:

1. Does it need to exist? Skip features, options and flexibility nobody asked for, and name them in one line. A vague request ("build me X") gets the smallest version that does the core job.
2. Already in this codebase (a helper, component, service, pattern)? Use it the way the surrounding code does.
3. Standard library or a platform feature? Use it, unless the project has its own. A house component beats a native widget.
4. An installed dependency? Use it. Never add a dependency for a few lines.
5. Can it be one line a reader gets at a glance? One line.
6. Otherwise: the minimum code that works.

- Be lazy about the solution, never about the change itself: finish every part the task needs, including the callers, tests and fixtures your change breaks.
- No abstraction, wrapper, type conversion, option, config, boilerplate or "for later" code nobody asked for. Keep values in the form the platform already gives you. Deletion beats addition. Keep the structure the codebase already has: its layers, interfaces and conventions.
- The shortest working diff wins, once you know everything it must touch. A one-liner that needs decoding is not short.
- Comment only the why the code cannot show, in one line.
- Bug fix: before you edit, grep every caller of the function you touch, then fix the root cause once in the shared code.
- Code you move or merge keeps its error handling and validation.
- Between options of equal size, take the one that is correct on edge cases.
- Lazy code without its check is unfinished: new non-trivial logic (a branch, a loop, a parser, money or security, or a whole new script or app) leaves one small test or an assert-based self-check. Trivial changes need none.
- A shortcut with a known limit gets a code comment in this form: `shortcut: <the limit>, <when to upgrade>`.

Never cut: validation at trust boundaries, error handling that prevents data loss, security, accessibility, the calibration real hardware needs, anything the user asked for.

## Levels

| Level | Behavior |
|-------|----------|
| **lite** | Build what was asked. Name the smaller option in one line and let the user pick. |
| **full** | The rules above. Default. |
| **ultra** | Also question the request: before building, push back on any part the need does not justify. |

   157	---
   158	
   159	# AGMSG-TASK dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01 — plan 024 P1-15 under D-024-17, D-024-19, D-024-20, D-024-21 and D-024-23
   160	
   161	## Goal
   162	
   163	The host is not a security boundary between seats sharing one OS user. gh keys Keychain credentials by host and user, so a worker running as the same macOS user can read the orchestrator's token regardless of `GH_CONFIG_DIR`. Prevention of a worker-initiated merge requires a separate OS user or host under D-024-23's future P1-16, which the operator decides whether to pursue. Until then the GitHub boundary is detection, not prevention. The regime rule requires the main-push CI run to flag a merge that lacks the required acceptance record or gate-passage evidence. P1-11 owns implementation of that flag; this task states the rule. Approvals and gate evidence of record are read from GitHub at a SHA through its API inside the sandbox. Host-local controls reduce accidental and injected damage without establishing isolation from a malicious same-UID process.
   164	
   165	This task implements section 5.14 items 1, 9 and 10 through a dedicated Claude worker configuration directory, static Edit deny rules on trusted paths and worktree configuration, managed sandbox enforcement, guarded project hooks, hook interpreter and PATH bindings, private worker caches and the rule that worker commands run inside their sandbox. Every Claude seat has `sandbox.failIfUnavailable: true`. Codex workers have `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`.
   166	
   167	The administrative managed policy is shared by every Claude seat. Its Herdr socket allowance and three control-plane exclusions remain available to workers as well as the orchestrator. Empty arrays in worker user settings do not subtract these managed entries. Their availability is a residual under item 0, and the worker execution rule supplies no authorization to use them outside the sandbox.
   168	
   169	[memory:decision] D-024-20 makes this task the dispatch prerequisite of P1-14 a05, P1-10 a02, audit-pool-a02 and P1-6. The orchestrator places this task in their `after:` fields before dispatch. This task has `after: []`. A Codex seat performs these Claude configuration edits under the routing rule, including the Worker Playbook step 4 sentence permitted by plan 021 step 8.3.
   170	
   171	The design source is `.orchestration/plans/024-design-quality.md` section 5.14 items 0 through 10. D-024-21, D-024-23 and the amended items 0, 1 and 8 govern this revision. D-024-22's proposed gh credential separation is superseded. The acceptance record preserves documentation facts and earlier proposals as history.
   172	
   173	The third C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-ecc7ab5.md.last.md`. Its five P1 findings remain recorded. The retained mechanisms are documented symlink-aware deny rules, approval reads outside the local object database, hook interpreter bindings and runtime hook failure policy.
   174	
   175	The fourth C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-8d42532.md.last.md`. Its six P1 findings remain recorded. The retained mechanisms are the static manifest deny inventory, protection of the orchestrator worklog and cache, narrow home denies around writable agmsg stores, correct linked-worktree hook placement and fixed hook-runtime PATH.
   176	
   177	The fifth C-80 text review is `.orchestration/validation/dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01-text-security-08e6ac7.md.last.md`. Its three P1 findings remain recorded. This revision removes the gh credential-separation mechanism, records the upstream agmsg store-driver residual and moves sandbox enforcement into administrative managed settings.
   178	
   179	The file-tool rule is the documented sentence, “Deny rules: apply when either the requested path or the file it resolves to matches.” Edit and Write refuse a requested file that is itself a symlink. Directory symlinks are subject to resolved-target permission checks, and the tools check the resolved location again when opening the approved file. These documented checks reduce redirection risk without establishing an atomic host security boundary against concurrent same-UID changes. [Claude permission reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/permissions#symlinks)
   180	
   181	The 2026-10-19T03:45:06Z sandbox probe ran `gh api repos/mryfmo/dotfiles --jq .name` and returned `dotfiles`. The recorded sandbox proxy used CONNECT without TLS interception, and gh read the Keychain token. This historical observation confirms neither credential separation nor final-head deployment readiness.
   182	
   183	The documentation facts recorded at 03:2xZ establish that Edit denies also affect the Bash sandbox, hooks reload during a session and an unavailable sandbox is fail-open by default. D-024-21's 04:0xZ decision establishes the dedicated Claude configuration mechanism, directory-specific Claude credentials, symlink coverage and `onFailure: "block"` support. These Claude credentials do not separate gh Keychain access.
   184	
   185	The settings documentation states, “In a worktree, it uses the file at the main checkout’s root.” Linked-worktree sessions therefore load the main checkout's `.claude/settings.local.json`. Worker hooks belong in `~/.claude-worker/settings.json`, and the main checkout's local hooks also receive the orchestrator-only guard. [Claude local settings reference](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/settings#where-claude-code-keeps-the-local-file-in-a-git-repository)
   186	
   187	## Files
   188	
   189	1. `home/dot_agents/agent-config.yaml` remains the source of agent policy and model profiles.
   190	
   191	   The manifest names `orchestrator_checkout` as the anchored primary checkout and supplies trusted deployment bindings for the guard, hook scripts and absolute interpreters. These bindings resolve outside worker worktrees and executable caches. Worker cwd, tool input, worktree configuration and inherited command variables do not select them. The manifest also supplies the trusted bindings from which the render resolves the absolute directories holding `ruff`, `prettier` and `terminal-browser`, without using mise shims.
   192	
   193	   The manifest defines a separate Claude worker settings output at `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-worker-settings.json` and an administrative managed-settings output at `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-managed-settings.json`. The worker configuration directory is `~/.claude-worker`. The launcher passes its expanded absolute path as `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`.
   194	
   195	   A static `trusted_paths:` list names every protected top-level path of the anchored checkout and the protected home paths. It explicitly includes `<anchored-checkout>/.agents/worklog/**` and `<anchored-checkout>/.agents/worklog/uv-cache/**`. The `.claude` entries protect tracked contents and the main checkout's `.claude/settings.local.json` without denying the whole `.claude/worktrees/` tree. The list explicitly names directory targets and descendant patterns where both are required.
   196	
   197	   Separate worker Edit denies protect `//<anchored-checkout>/.claude/worktrees/*/.claude/**` and `//<anchored-checkout>/.claude/worktrees/*/.mcp.json`. The `.claude` directory target is also protected. Ordinary worktree source files and the per-seat cache remain outside these configuration denies.
   198	
   199	   The home-path denies are `~/.agents/agent-config.yaml`, `~/.agents/model-profiles.env`, `~/.agents/skills/**/scripts/**`, `~/.agents/skills/**/SKILL.md`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins`, `~/.claude/**`, `~/.claude-worker/**`, `~/.local/bin/**`, `~/.local/share/**`, `~/.local/state/**`, `~/.local/share/mise/**`, `~/.config/**`, `~/.cache/uv/**` and `~/.local/share/uv/**`. No blanket deny on `~/.agents/**` or `~/.local/**` replaces this list.
   200	
   201	   The agmsg store paths `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/teams`, `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/run` and `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/ext-tools` remain writable. Deny patterns cover neither these directory targets nor their descendants except the file `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins`. Edit denies merge into the sandbox configuration, so Bash writes to that file and `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**` are refused too. Their influence on installed delivery-script driver and plugin selection remains the separately owned agmsg residual.
   202	
   203	   The orchestrator retains its existing permissions, cache allowances and hook functions within the administrative managed policy. Its user baseline receives `failIfUnavailable: true`, `onFailure: "block"` on its hooks and the interpreter and PATH bindings below. Its exclusion entries match the three control-plane entries specified below; the former Linux `gh *` exclusion is removed.
   204	
   205	   The worker user policy contains no excluded commands and sets `allowUnsandboxedCommands: false`. It contains no Herdr socket allowance and no allow-all Unix socket switch. Its sandbox is enabled and has `failIfUnavailable: true`. Shared managed entries remain effective despite these empty worker arrays.
   206	
   207	   Worker `sandbox.filesystem.allowWrite` contains only the existing agmsg store paths and per-seat cache paths. The worktree is the project root and supplies its ordinary project write root. Shared uv and mise write allowances are not copied into worker settings.
   208	
   209	   The Codex worker profile sets `sandbox_workspace_write.network_access=true`. Existing model selections and Codex shared Git grants retain their meaning. The deterministic Codex permgate rule that stops approving sandbox escapes is an operator step.
   210	
   211	2. `scripts/generate-agent-configs.py` renders the distinct orchestrator user, worker user and administrative managed baselines from the manifest.
   212	
   213	   The trusted-path deny inventory is rendered from the static `trusted_paths:` list. Rendering performs no Git object traversal, `git ls-tree` enumeration or tracked-path discovery. Worker-writable refs, replacement refs and objects do not determine the list.
   214	
   215	   Anchored checkout rules use absolute `//` permission patterns. Rendering includes the explicitly listed trusted paths themselves and their descendants. It emits no checkout-wide parent deny that defeats ordinary worktree writes. The separate worktree-configuration denies remain present.
   216	
   217	   Edit denies cover Write, MultiEdit and NotebookEdit through Claude's permission system. Requested-path and resolved-target matching supply the documented symlink coverage. The generator creates no `executable_worker-write-boundary.py` or replacement PreToolUse path validator.
   218	
   219	   Every direct Git verification introduced or changed by this task follows section 5.14 item 2. It begins with `env -i`, uses the selected absolute Git executable and an explicit `-C`, sets `GIT_CONFIG_NOSYSTEM=1` and `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=/dev/null`, and supplies `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null -c core.fsmonitor=false -c protocol.allow=never -c protocol.https.allow=always`. Diff and show also use `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` and `-c core.attributesFile=/dev/null -c core.sshCommand=false`.
   220	
   221	   The private executable-link directory contains only links to selected absolute trusted binaries. Rendering rejects missing bindings, worker-controlled executable paths, shims, empty PATH entries and relative PATH entries. Python invocations use `-I`. Hook execution uses the role-specific pinned PATH bindings specified below. The render test asserts that `ruff`, `prettier` and `terminal-browser` resolve under the rendered orchestrator tool PATH on the rendering host and that no entry of that PATH is a shims directory.
   222	
   223	   `tests/unit/test_generate_agent_configs.py::GenerateAgentConfigsTest::test_worker_deny_inventory_is_static_and_matches_manifest` asserts that the rendered list equals the manifest entry after trusted anchor and home expansion. It explicitly asserts the rendered home-path deny patterns `~/.local/share/**` and `~/.local/state/**`, including the application code under `~/.local/share` used by `~/.local/bin` wrappers such as `terminal-browser`. It reads the filesystem of the test's own checkout and asserts that every tracked top-level source path is represented. It invokes no Git command to discover those paths.
   224	
   225	   The inventory fixtures cover files, directories, `.claude` children, the main-local settings file, ordinary worktree writes, both worktree-configuration patterns, an anchor containing spaces and both explicit worklog patterns. Adding a top-level source path without a manifest entry makes the coverage assertion fail. Planted Git environment and replacement-ref fixtures leave rendering unchanged.
   226	
   227	   `tests/unit/test_claude_worker_settings.py::ClaudeWorkerSettingsTest::test_worker_denies_leave_agmsg_stores_writable` asserts the rendered deny patterns `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/plugins/**` and `~/.agents/skills/agmsg/db/trusted-plugins` and verifies that the four store directories `db`, `teams`, `run` and `ext-tools` and their descendant fixtures remain writable except that one file.
   228	
   229	3. `home/.chezmoitemplates/claude-settings-managed.json` remains the orchestrator user baseline.
   230	
   231	   The existing `home/dot_claude/modify_private_settings.json` continues to render the orchestrator's `~/.claude/settings.json`. That modify script is not imported into the dedicated worker renderer and remains outside `allowed_files`.
   232	
   233	   This existing template's name does not make it administrative managed settings. The new `claude-managed-settings.json` template supplies that separate scope.
   234	
   235	   The orchestrator retains its current user policy content apart from `failIfUnavailable: true`, `onFailure: "block"`, the required interpreter and PATH bindings and alignment of exclusions with the three control-plane entries. Worker-specific Edit denies and restricted worker cache allowances do not enter this baseline. Administrative sandbox requirements apply to the orchestrator as well as workers.
   236	
   237	   Every generated hook entry carries `onFailure: "block"`. Every generated command hook starts with `/usr/bin/env -i PATH=<pinned> HOME=<rendered home> LANG=C.UTF-8 TERM=dumb CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR="${CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR}" CLAUDE_ENV_FILE="${CLAUDE_ENV_FILE}"` before its selected absolute interpreter and script path. The formatter and project-map hooks run through the installed orchestrator-only guard and receive the role-specific pinned PATH specified below. Merge evidence identifies hooks appended by the existing modify script and distinguishes them from generated baseline entries. The report accounts for the effective deployed hook set without equating baseline coverage with coverage of runtime-added entries.
   238	
   239	   `tests/unit/test_claude_settings_merge.py::ClaudeSettingsMergeTest::test_orchestrator_settings_keep_existing_policy` compares the merged policy with the preceding baseline and accounts for the specified changes. Repeated merging remains idempotent and preserves unrelated settings.
   240

   394	
   395	## Out of scope
## Codex seat worklogs

Project layouts vary by language. Set up this worklog structure only when it
does not already exist, and use timestamped filenames in `YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS`
form:

- `.agents/worklog/codex/plan/<timestamp>_plan.md` stores the plan and design
  written before implementation. Ask the user questions when needed, and
  update the plan when questions, learning, or completed tasks change it. It
  must contain `Goal`, `Scope`, `Assumptions`, `Design`, `Tests`, and
  `Open Questions`.
- `.agents/worklog/codex/todo/<timestamp>_todo.md` derives its tasks from the
  plan. Move completed items from `TODO` to `Done`; when `TODO` is empty, set
  its status to `done` and rename it to `<timestamp>_done.md`. It must contain
  `TODO` and `Done`.
- `.agents/worklog/codex/learn/<timestamp>_learn.md` records only reusable,
  validated knowledge that speeds a future decision. State what was learned
  and where it applies, update the plan's `Assumptions`, `Design`, or `Tests`
  when relevant. A learn file must contain `Date`, `Learnings`, and
  `Plan Updates`.

Every plan, todo, and learn file starts with YAML frontmatter containing
`type` (`plan`, `todo`, or `learn`), `id` (`YYYYMMDD_HHMMSS`), `owner` (for
example, `codex-a`), and ISO8601 `created_at` and `updated_at`. Additionally:

- todo requires `status`, `workstream`, and `related_plan`; status is one of
  `active`, `blocked`, `done`, or `superseded`;
- plan requires `status`, one of `draft`, `active`, `done`, or `superseded`;
- learn requires `validated` (`true` or `false`) and `apply_to` (plan/tests),
  and may be created only when reusable and validated.

Optional frontmatter keys are `depends_on` (todo ID array), `blocked_reason`
for blocked work, `evidence` (path array), and `tags`.

# Acceptance: dotfiles-T133-worker-write-boundary-a01 (plan 024 P1-15: the worker write boundary; D-024-17/19/20)

- text: review-profile design 03:18Z (prompt `.agents/worklog/orchestrator-glue/p115-a01-design-prompt.md`); checker OK; 14 files; not dispatched.
